का आ 1999 --- बैककारी विनियमन अधिनियम 1944 
( 1849 मा 10 ) की धाग 53 ताग प्रदम शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते 
हए, केन्द्रीय मरकार, भारतीय रिजर्व बैंक की सिफारिण पर सवाग 
मोषणा करती है कि उसन अधिनियम की धारा 10- 7 की उपधाग 
( 1 ) तथा ( 2 ) के उपबध नमिलनाड मग्नेन्टाइन अंक लि टीकाग्नि 
पर 18 अन 1992 से 17 सितम्बर, 1992 तक तीन महीने की 
अवधि के मान सवा बैंक के नियमित पूर्णकालिक अध्यक्ष को निगमित 
होने सक , इसमे में जो भी पहले ही , नाग नही होगे । 


नई दिल्ली, 10 जुलाई, 1992 
का पा 2001 -- ~ बैंककारी विनियमन अधिनियम , 1949 ( 1940 
का 10 ) की धाग 53 धाग प्रदम शक्तियो का प्रयोग करते हुए ,

मा . प्रा . 2014. -- यत: केन्द्रीय सरकार को यह प्रतीत होता है 
लोकहित में यह पावरफफ है कि गुजरात राज्य में कलोल क्षेत्र में 
ओ . जी . एस .- IV से जी . जी . एस . - V तक पेट्रोलियम के परिवहन के लिये 
पाइप लाइन तेल तथाप्राक गामा वारा बिछाई जानी चाहिए । 

और यतः यह प्रतीत होता है कि ऐगी लाइनों को बिछाने के 
प्रयोजन के लिए एनपामञ्च अनुषी गे पणित भूमि में उपयोग फा 
माधिकार अजित करना प्रावश्यक है । 

मत: भय पेट्रोलियम और पनिज पाइपलाइन ( भूमि मे उपयोग के 
अधिकार का प्रजन ) अधिनियम , 1962 ( 1962 का 50 ) की धारा 
3की उपधारा ( 1 ) द्वारा प्रदत शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते हुए केन्द्रीय सरकार 
में उसमें उपयोग का प्राधिकार अजित करने का अपना भाशय एतद्वारा 
पापित किया है । 

बशर्ते कि उक्त भूमि में हिनबस कोई व्यक्ति ; उस भूमि के नीने 
पपलाइन बिछाने के लिए प्राोग समक्ष प्राधिकारी तेन गया प्राकृतिक 
पास भागोग , निर्माण और दखभाल प्रभाग , मकरपुरा रोड, बड़ौदा- ) 
कोपरा रक्षिसत्तमा की तारीख से 21 दिन के भीतर कर सकेगा । 

र ऐसा मासंप करते थाना हर प्यो विनिमिष्टता यह भी कपन 
करेगा कि मया या यह चाहता है कि उसकी सुपाई यक्तिगत रूप से 
हो या किसी विधि व्यवसायी को मात ।

कि लोकहित में यह भावश्यक है कि गुजरात राज्य में जो . जो . एस . - VI

प्रयोजन के लिए एतद्पाबद्ध अनुसूचो में वर्णित भूमि में उपयोग का 
अधिकार अजित करना आवश्यक है ।

का . मा . 2020 -~ ~यतः केन्द्रीय सरकार को यह प्रतीत 
होता है कि लोकहित में यह आवश्यक है कि 
गुजरात राज्य में जी . जी . एम . 5 से जी . जी . एस . 6 तक 
पेट्रोलियम के परिवहन के लिये पाइपलाइन तेल तया प्राकृतिक गैस 
आयोग द्वारा बिछाई जानी चाहिए । 

और अतः यह प्रतीत होता है कि ऐसी लाइनों को बिछाने के 
प्रयोजन के लिए एतदपाबद्ध अनुसूची में वर्णित भूमि में उपयोग का 
अधिकार अजित करना आवश्यक हैं । 

अतः अब पेट्रोलियम और खनिज पाइपलाइन ( भूमि में उपयोग के 
अधिकार का अर्जन ) अधिनियम , 1962 ( 1962 का 50 ) की धारा 
3 की उपधारा ( 1 ) द्वारा प्रदत्त शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते हुए केन्द्रीय सरकार 
ने उसमें उपयोग का अधिकार अर्जित करने का अपना प्राशय एतद्वारा 
घोषित किया है । 
__ बशर्ते कि उक्त भूमि में हितबद्ध कोई व्यक्ति , उस भूमि के नीचे 
पाइपलाइन बिछाने के लिए प्राक्षेप सक्षम प्राधिकारी तेल तथा प्राकृतिक 
गैस प्रायोग, निर्माण और देखमाल प्रभाग , मकरघुरा रोड, बडोदा ~ , 
को इस अधिसूचना को तारीख से 21 दिनों के भीतर कर सकेगा ।

करेगा को क्या यह बह चाहता है कि उसकी सुनवाई व्यक्तिगत रूप से हा 
या किसी विधी व्यवसायो की मार्फत ।

New Delhi, the 14th July , 1992 
S. O . 2030. - Whereas it appears to the Central Government 
that it is necessary in the public intcrest that for the 
transport of the petroleum from PDAH to Padror EPS in 
Gujarat State pipeline should be laid by the Oil & Natural 
Gas Commission . 

And whereas it appears that for the purpose of laying 
such pipeline, it is necesary to acquire that right of user 
in the land described in the schedulo annexed here to - 
____ Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by 
sub -section ( 1 ) of the section 3 of the petroloum and Mine 
rals pipelines (Acquisition of Right of User in the land ) 

Act, 1962 (50 of 1962 ), the Central Government hereby dec 
lares it s intention to acquire the right of user therein , 

Provided that any person interested in the said land may , 
within 21 days from the date of this notification , 
to the laying of the pipelino under the land to the Compo 
tent Authority , Oil & Natural Gas Commission, Construction 
and Maintenance Division , Makarpura Road Vado 
dara - 390009 . 

And every person making such an objection shall also 
state specifically whether he wishes to be heard in person 
or by legal Practitioner .

का , प्रा . 2030 -- - यत. केन्द्रीय सरकार को यह प्रतीत होता है कि 
लोकहित में यह प्रावश्यक है कि गुजरात राज्य में पी डी ए एच से पावरा 
ई पी एस तक पेट्रोलियम के परिवहन के लिये पाइपलाईन तेल तथा प्राकृतिक 
गैस पायोग द्वारा बिछाई जानी चाहिए । 

और, यस , यह प्रतीत होता है कि ऐसी लाइनों को मिछाने के प्रयोजन 
के लिए एतद्पायर अनुसूची में वर्णित भूमि में उपयोग का अधिकार 
पणित करना प्रावश्यक है । 

प्रतः, अब, पेट्रोलियम और खनिज पाइपलाईन ( भूमि में उपयोग के 
अधिकार का पर्जन ) अधिनियम, 1962 ( 1962 का 50 ) की धारा 3 की 
उपधारा द्वारा प्रवस्त शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते हुए केन्द्रीय सरकार ने उसमें 
उपयोग का अधिकार अजित करने का प्रपना आशय एतद्वारा घोषित 
किया है । 

बशर्ते कि उक्त भूमि में हितब कोई व्यक्ति ; उस भूमि के नीचे 
पाइपलाइन बिछामे के लिए प्राक्षेप समक्ष प्राधिकारी सेल तथा प्राकृतिक गैस 
प्रायोग, निर्माण और देखभाल प्रभाग , मकरपुरा रोड, बड़ी - को हम 
मधिसूचना की तारीख से 21 दिनों के भीतर कर सकेगा । 

और ऐसा आक्षेप करने वाला हर व्यक्ति विनिर्दिष्टतः यह भी कयन 
करेगा कि क्या वह यह चाहता है कि उसकी सुनवाई व्यक्तिगत रूप से 
हो या फिसी विधि व्यवसायी की मार्फत ।

का . प्रा . 2031. -- यत: केन्द्रीय सरकार को यह प्रतीत होता है कि

एस तक पेट्रोलियम के परिवहन के लिये पाइपलाइन तेय तया प्राकृतिक गैस 
आयोग द्वारा विछाई जानी चाहिए । 

और, यतः, यह प्रतीत होता है कि ऐमो लाइनों को बिछाने के प्रयोजन 
के लिए एतद्पाबद अनुसूची में वणित भूमि में उपयोग का अधिकार जित 
करना आवश्यक है । 

भतः , अब , पेट्रोलियम और खनिज पाइपलाईन ( भूमि में उपयोग के 
मधिकार का प्रर्जन ) अधिनियम , 1962 ( 1962 का 50 ) की धारा 3 की

उपयोग का अधिकार मजित करने का प्रपना भाशय एतद्वारा घोषित 
किया है । 

बशर्ते कि उक्त भूमि में हितब कोई व्यक्ति , उस भूमि के नीचे 
पाइपलाइन बिछाने के लिए प्राक्षेप ममा प्राधिकारी ने तथा प्राकृतिक गैस

अधिसूचना की तारीख से 21 दिन के भीतर कर सकेगा । 
____ और ऐसा प्राक्षेप करने वाला हर व्यक्ति विनिर्दिष्टा . यह भी कथन

या किसी विधि व्यवसायी की मार्फन ।

पा आ 03 : - - पत केन्द्रीय सरकार को यह प्रतीत होता है कि 
लोकहित में यह प्रावश्यक है कि गुजरात राज्य मे इम्यू रम्य टी पी 
से डाडर नवी तक पेट्रोलियम के परिवहन के लिये पाइपलाईन तेम तथा 
प्राकृतिक गैम पायोग द्वारा बिछाई जानी चाहिए । 

और रन , यह प्रतीत होता है कि ऐसी लाइनो को बिछाने के प्रयोजन 
के लिए एनद पाबद्ध प्रमसूची में वर्णित भूमि में उपयोग का अधिकार प्रजिन 
करमा आवश्यक है । 

अन , अध , पेट्रोलियम और खनिज पाइपलाईम ( भूमि मे उपयोग के 
मधिकार का अर्जन ) अधिनियम, 1962 ( 1962 का 50 ) की धारा 3 की उप 
घाग द्वारा प्रदम शक्तिया का प्रयोग करते हुए केन्द्रीय सरकार ने उममे उपयोग 
का अधिकार अजित करने का अपना प्रशय एतद्द्वारा घोषित किया है । 

बशत कि उक्म भूमि में हितबद्ध कोई व्यक्ति , उम भूमि के नीचे 
पाइपलाइन बिछाने के लिए माक्षेप समक्ष प्राधिकारी तेल तथा प्राकृतिक गैस 
मायोग , निर्माण और देखभाल प्रभाग, मकरपुरा रोड, बडोदा ) को इस 
अधिसूचना की सारीख मे 21 दिनों के भीतर कर सकेगा । 

और ऐसा प्राक्षेप करने वाला हर व्यक्ति विनिर्दिष्ट न: यह भो कथन 
करेगा कि क्या वह यह चाहता है कि उसकी सुनवाई व्यक्तिगत रूप से 
हो या किमी विधि व्यवसायी की मार्फत ।

का . प्रा . 20 36. -- प्रतः केन्द्रीय सरकार को यह प्रतीत होता है कि 
लोकहित में यह आवश्यक है कि गुजरात राज्य मे माडा - 1 मे जी एन ए 
क्षु तक पेट्रोलियम के परिवहन के लिये पाइपलाइन तेल तथा प्राकृतिक 
गैस आयोग द्वारा बिछाई जानी चाहिए । 

और अतः यह प्रतीत होता है कि ऐसी लाइनो को बिछाने के प्रयोजन 
के लिए तपाबद्ध अनुसूची में वर्णित भूमि में उपयोग का अधिकार 
अजित करना प्रावश्यक है । 

अतः अब पेट्रोलियम और खनिज पाइपलाइन ( भूमि में उपयोग के 
अधिकार का अर्जन ) अधिनियम , 1962 ( 1962 का 50 ) की धारा 3 
की उपधारा द्वारा प्रदत्त शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते हुए केन्द्रीय सरकार ने 
उममें उपयोग का अधिकार अर्जित करने का अपना प्राशय एतद्द्वारा 
घोषित किया है । 

बशर्ते मि उक्त भूमि में हिमबद कोई व्यक्मि , उम भीम के नीचे 
पारपलाइन बिछाने के लिए प्राक्षेप मक्षम प्राधिकारी तेल तथा प्राकृतिक 
गैस पायोग, निर्माण और देमभान प्रभाग , मकररंग रोड , बड़ौवा - 9 को 
इम अधिसूचना की तारीख मे 21 दिनो के भीतर कर सकेगा । 

और ऐसा आक्षेप करने वाला हर व्यक्ति विनिर्दिष्टस यह भी कथन 
करेगा कि क्या यह बह चाहता है कि उपकी सुनवाई व्यक्तिगत म्प से 
हो या किसी विधि व्यवसायी की मार्फत ।

पायोग द्वारा बिछाई जानी चाहिए । 

और प्रतः यह प्रतीत होता है कि ऐसी लाइनों को बिछाने के प्रयोजन 
के लिए एतद्पापा मम सूची , में वणित भूमि में उपयोग का अधिकार 
मजित करमा प्रावश्यक है ।

भधिकार का पर्मन ) मधिनियम , 1962 ( 1962 का 50 ) की धारा

घोषित किया है । 

बार्ते कि उक्त भूमि में हितबब कोई व्यक्ति ; उम भूमि के नीचे

S. O . 2041 . - Whereas it appears to the Central Government 
that it is necessary in the public interest that for the 
transport of the petroleum from GGS - I to Jn . Point in 
Gujarat State pipeline should be laid by the Oil & Natural 
Gas Commission, 

And whereas it appears that for the purpose of laying 
such pipeline, it is necessary to acquired that right of user 
in the land described in the schedule annexed hereto . . 


का . प्रा . 2041. - - यतः केन्द्रीय सरकार को यह प्रतीत होता है कि लोक 
हित में यह आवश्यक है कि गुजरात राज्य में जी . जी . एस . ~ 1 से 
सम्मम बिन्दु तक पेट्रोलियम के परिवहन के लिये पाइपलाइन तेल मया 
प्राकृतिक गैस प्रायोग द्वारा बिछाई जानी चाहिए । 

और माता पर प्रतीत होता है कि ऐमी लाइनो को बिछाने के प्रयोजन 
के लिए एतदपाबद्ध मनुसूची में वणित भूमि में उपयोग का अधिकार जित 
करना प्रावश्यक है । 


Now , therefore, in exercise of the powers cooferred by 
sub - section ( 1) of the section 3 of the Petroleum and Mine 
rals pipelines ( Acquisition of Right of User in the land ) Act , 
1962 ( 50 of 1962 ) , the Cleotral Government bereby declares 
it s intention to acquire the right of user thereiit 


[ भाग [[ - - संर 3( ii ) ] 


भारत का राप्रपत्र : प्रगस्त 1, 1992 श्रावण 10, 2014 


3249 : 


Prov .ded that any person interested in the said land may , 
within 21 days from the date of this notification , object to 
tho laying of the pipeline under the land to the Competent 
Authority , Oil & Natural Gas Commission , Construction & 
Maintenance Division , Makarpura Road, Vadodara -390009. 

And every person making such an objection shall also 
state specifically whether he wishes to be heard in person or 
by legal Practitioner .

का . आ . 2043 . -- जबकि केन्द्र सरकार यह अनुभव करती है कि सार्वजनिक हित में यह पावश्यक है कि पैट्रोलियम पदार्थ 
एवं प्राकृतिक गैस लाने के लिए मान्य प्रदेश में पाइप लाइन परियोजना के अंतर्गत पासलपुरि - 9 से पासलपुरि - 8 तक तेल और 
प्राकृतिक गैस पायोग द्वारा बिछाया जाना है । 

और यह भी अनुभव करती हैं कि उस कार्य के लिए इसके माथ संलग्न विवरणी में निर्धारित भूमि पर प्रयोक्ता का अधिकार 
ग्रहण करना मावश्यक है । 

प्रतः पेट्रोलियम एवं खनिज पाइप लाइन भूमि पर प्रयोक्ता का अधिकार ग्रहण अधिनियम 1962 ( 1962 का 50 ) 
के खण्ड 3 के उपखण्ड ( 1 ) द्वारा प्रदत्त शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते हुए केन्द्र सरकार एतबारा उम पर प्रयोक्ता का अधिकार 
ग्रहण करने की आशा की घोषणा करती है । 

बशर्ते कि उक्त भमि में अपनी रुचि रखने वाला कोई भी व्यक्ति अधिसूचना की तारीख से 21 दिन के भीतर भूमिगत 
पाइप लाइन बिछाने के विरोध में अपनी प्रापसि सक्षम प्राधिकारी तेल और प्राकृतिक गैस पायोग, के . जि , प्रोजेक्ट , 
भूसेकरणा , कार्यालय , राजमंनि , प्रांध्रप्रदेश में दर्ज करा सकता है । 

और ऐसी प्रापसि दर्ज कराते समय किसी भी व्यक्ति को यह विशेष रूप से निर्दिष्ट करना होगा कि वह व्यक्तिगत 
रूप से अथवा विधिव्यवसायक के माध्यम से अपना मत प्रस्तुत करना चाहता है ।

का . आ . 2044: - -- जबकि केन्द्र मरकार यह अनुभव करती है कि सार्वजनिक हित में यह आवश्यक है कि पैट्रो 
लियम पदार्थ एवं प्राकृतिक गैस लाने के लिए पान्ध्र प्रदेश में पाइप लाइन परियोजना के अंतर्मत पोरी 2 में इपीएस पोरी तक

ग्रहण करने की मंशा की घोषणा करती है ।

का . आ . 20 45. .. - जबकि केन्द्र सरकार यह अनुभव करती है कि सार्वजनिक हित में यह आवश्यक है कि पेट्रोलियम पदार्थ एवं 
प्राकृतिक गैस लाने के लिए मान्ध्र प्रदेश में पाइपलाइन परियोजना के अंतर्गत चिन्तलपल्ली 1 से 14 " टी पाइन्ट तक तेल 
और प्राकृतिक गैम आयोग भाग बिछाया जाना है । 

और यह भी अनुभव करती है कि उस कार्य के लिए इसके साथ संलग्न विवरणी में निर्धारित भूमि पर प्रयोक्ता का 
अधिकार ग्रहण करना आवश्यक है । 

प्रत. पेट्रोलियम एवं खनिज पाइप लाइन भूमि पर प्रयोक्ता का अधिकार ग्रहण अधिनियम 1962 ( 1962 का 50 ) के 
खण्ड 3 के उपखण्ड ( 1 ) द्वारा प्रदत्त शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते हुए केन्द्र सरकार एतदद्वारा उस पर प्रयोक्त का अधिकार 
ग्रहण करने की अंशा की घोषणा करती है । 

बशर्ते कि उक्त भूमि में अपनी रुचि रखने वाला कोई भी व्यक्ति अधिसूचना की तारीख से 21 दिन के भीतर भूमि 
गत पाइप लाइन बिछाने के विरोध में अपनी आपसि सक्षम प्राधिकार तेल और प्राकृतिक गैस आयोग प्रोजेक्ट , भूसंस्करण , 
कार्यालय, राजमंद्रि , प्रान्ध्रप्रदेश में दर्ज करा सकता है । 

और एसी आपत्ति दर्ज कराते समय किसी भी व्यक्ति को यह विशेष रूप से निर्दिष्ट करना होगा कि वह व्यक्तिगत 
रूप से प्रथवा विधि व्यवसाय के माध्यम से अपना मत प्रस्तुत करना चाहता है ।

के लिये एतत्वारा अनुसूची में वर्णिम भूमि में उपयोग का अधिकार 
अर्जित करना प्रावश्यक है ।

मसः मन्त्र परोनियम और खनिज पाईप लाइन ( भूमि में उपयोग के 
अधिकार का पौर ) माधनियम , 1962 ( 1962 का 50 ) की धाग 3 
को उपधारा ( 1 ) द्वारा प्रदत्त शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते हुए केन्द्रीय सरकार 
ने उममें उपयोग का अधिकार अमित करने का अपना भाशय एतद्वारा 
घोषित किया है । 

चशतं कि उक्त भूमि में हितब कोई व्यक्ति , उस भूमि के मीचे 
पाइप लाइन बिछाने के लिये प्राक्षेप उपायुक्त , शिवमागर / जोरहाट , असम के 
कार्यालय में इस अधिसूचमा की तारीख के 21 दिनों के भीतर कर सकेगा । 

और ऐमा भाक्षेप करने वाला हर व्यक्ति विनिर्विष्टस: यह भी कथन 
फरेगा कि क्या वह यह चाहता है कि उसकी सुनवाई व्यक्तिगत हो या 
किसी विधि व्यवमायी की मार्फत ।

और अतः यह प्रतीत होता है कि ऐमी लाइनों को बिछाने के प्रयोजन 
के लिए एतपायस अनधि में वर्णित भूमि म उपयोग का अधिकार 
अजिम करना प्रावश्यक है । 

प्रत प्रब पेट्रोलियम और खनिज पाइपलाइन ( ममि में उपयोग के 
अधिकार का अर्जन ) अधिनियम , 1962, ( 1962 का 50 ) की धारा 3 
की उपधारा ( 1 ) द्वारा प्रदत शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते हुए केन्द्रीय सरकार 
ने उममे उपयोग का अधिकार अजित करने का अपना पापाय एसद्वारा 
घोषित किया है । 

बशन कि उका ममि में हितबर कोई व्यक्ति, उस भूमि के नीचे 
पाइप लाइन बिछाने के लिए प्राक्षेप सक्षम प्राधिकारी, तेल तथा प्राकृतिक 
गैस आयोग, निर्माण और देखभाल प्रभाग , मकरपुरा रोड़, बडोवा- 9, को 
इम अधिसूचना को सारोख से 21 दिनों के भीतर कर सकेगा । 

और ऐसा माक्षेप करने वाला हर व्यक्ति विनिर्दिष्टतः यह भी कथन 
करेगा कि क्या यह वह चाहता है कि उसकी सुनवाई व्यक्तिगत रूप से 
हो या किसी विधि व्यवसायी को मार्फत ।

अधिमोगियों को बेदखली ) अधिनियम , 1971 ( 1971 का 40 ) को

साम्भ ( 1 ) मे उल्लिरिनप भधिकार का , जा कानुनी प्राधिकरण का एक 
मधिकारी है, और सरकार के मालित अधिकारी को पंमित में ममता 
अधिकार है , उमा अधिनियम के प्रयोजन के लिए मंपदा अधिकारी नियुक्त 
फरसी है, जो उक्त अनुसूची के स्तम्भ ( 2 ) मे को तत्स्थिामी प्रविष्टि में 
विनिविष्ट प्रवर्गों के सरकारी स्थानो के मबंध में प्रपनो अधिकारिकता की 
स्थानीय मीमामों के भीतर. उक्त प्रधिनियम धाग या उसके अधीन मंपया 
अधिकारी को बरत शक्तियों का प्रयाग और उस पर अधिरोपित कस्तट्यो 
का पालन करेगा ।

AWARD 
This is a reference made by the Central Government us 
10 ( 1 ) ( d ) of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 ( 14 of 1947 ) 
for adjudication of the dispute as to whether the action of 
the management of MS. S . Lal and Co . ( Qubsequently re 
named as M / s . Essel Mining and Industries Ltd . ) , owner 
of Julling Longalota Iron Ming denying employment to Smt. 
Mali Dei, Water Carrier w .e. f. 12 - 10 -79 is justified . 

2 . It is needless to make it reference to the pleading of 
the parties as becase the same have been succintly stated by 
this Tribunal in the earlier order dated 7 - 3 - 87 . 

On filing of the written statement by the partics, heering 
on mrit was taker up . Whilc challenging the maintainability 
of the referçace the management pleaded that Smt, Mali Dei 
was never employed as a workmun under it. On an appraisal 
of the evidence adduced by the partie , this Tribunal while 
deciding the reference maintainable held that the wolkınan 
was an employee of the management and that she was refus 
od of employment. However , the reference could not be 
fully and finally disposed of and award passed as evidence 
was lacking as to whether the workman hud been painfully 
omployed subsequent to her disengagement. Feeling aggrieved 
by this order the management moved the Hon ble High 
Court in a writ which was dismissed in 1991. As the Hon ble 
High Court directed to dispose of the case within three 
months from the date of appearancc of the parties, they 
wore given opportunity to lead cvidence on the question 
of gainful employment. As the inanagement did not 
turn -up the workman gave evidence whereafter argument was 
heard and date was fixed for passing award . Subsequently 
the management appeared and prayed to allow it to 
participate in the hearing before passing award . Having 
heard the parties order was passed allowing the management 
to have its say before final adjudication of the dispute . There 
ufter the management cross- examined the workman examined 
carlior and also examined one witness on its behalf. 

3 . It is contended on behalf of management that in 
view of the observation of the Hon ble Supreme Court this 
Tribunal ought to have allowed it to lead ovidence both 
on the question of maintainability of the reference as well 
as on merit. That having not bcen done all the issues 
involved in the reference cannot be answercd in either way . 
I am not propared to accept such contention of the manage 
ment for the reasons set -out below 

The Anding dated 7 -3 -87 of this Tribunal on two issues 
as aforesaid was challenged by the management in the 
Hon ble High Court by way of writ . Having heard the 
parties, their I ordships refused to interfere with the findings 
and dismissed the writ . Against tho said order of the 
Hon ble Court, the management Aled a special leave petition 
before the Apex Court, From the xerox copy of the order 
of the Hon ble Supreme Court , it transpires that the manage 
ment withdrew the said special leave petition . While allowing 
the prayer for withdrawal the Hon ble Supreme Court 
observed that it is open to the parties to agitato whatever 
issues here are in the reference before the appropriate Court . 
The management intending to take advantage of this observa 
tion submitted that by necessary implication the Hon ble 
Supreme Court has set- aside the judgement passed by the 
Hon ble High Court. This submission , 19 I have already said , 
can not be accepted . Jf at all the managemont had valid legal 
points to challenge the findings of the Hon ble Courut the 
speciel leave petition would have been allowed . From the 
aforesaid observation of the Hon ble Supreme Court what I 
gather is that it is for the partics to agitate the remaining 
issue which is yet to be decided by this Tribunal. 

4 . From the available evidence it is now to be seen 
Whcther denial of employment to the workman is legal and 
justified The management on whom onus lies to prove 
the same did not choose to lead any evidence in that 
regard . On the other hand , the workman in her evidence 
has given out that as she complained to the Labour Officer 
regarding less payment of wages the management removed

AWARD 
This is a reference made by the Central Government u / s 
10X1 ) ( ) of the Industrial Disputos Act, 1947 ( 14 of 1947) 
for adjudication of the dispute as to whether the action of 
the management of Jiling Longalota Iron Mines of M / s . 
S . Lal and Co . Ltd . ( subsequently renamed as M / s. Essel 
Mining and jpdustries Ltd . ) denying employment to Sri 
Bharat Mabanto , Water Carrier with effect from 7 - 9 -79 is 
justified . 


Nos. 2 and 3 . In view of such evidence , I am inclined to 
hold that the action of the management in doing away with 
the job of the workman has no sanction under law and 
hence the same has to be held to be illegal and unjusti 
fied . 

5. The next question that falls for consideration is 
whether after being denied of service tho workman way 
employed elsewhere or remained unemployed , 

The workman in his evidence has stated that being 
denied of employment since 7 - 9 -79 he is unemployed . On 
tho other hand , it is the case of the managment that the 
workman has been working under a contractor of Kalinga 
Mining Corporation as a Mizoor. To prove this the 
management has examined MW - 2 who during cross - exami 
nation would admit that he only heard from the villagers 
about the emplcyment of the workman in the aforesaid 
Mines. MW - 3 denied to have any knowledge regarditt 

employmght of the workman elsewhere . This being tho 
sum total of evidenco led by the parties, I am inclined 
to hold that since disengagement by the management the 
workman has remained unemployed . 


2 . It is needless to muke & reference to the plcadings of 
the parties as bccausc the game have been succially stated 
by this Tribunal in the earlier order dated 9 - 3 -87. 


On filing of the written statement by the parties , hearing 
on merit was taken -up . While challenging the maintainability 
of the re - orence the management pleaded that Sri Bharat 
Mabanto was never employed as a workman under it . ON 
an appraisal of the evidence adduced by the parties this 
Tribunal while deciding the referenco maintainable held 
that Sri Mahanto Way an employee of the management 
and that his services were terminated . However , the 
jeforence could not be fully and finally disposed of and 
award passed as evidence was lacking as to whether the 
wothan hitd been gainfully employed subsequent to his 
disengagement, Fooling aggrived by this order the manage 
ment moved the Hon ble High Court in a writ which 
was dismissed in 1991 . As the Hon ble Court directed] 
to dispose of the case withil three nonths from the 
date of appearance of the parties , they were given 
opportunity to Icad evidence on the question of gainful 
cinploracnt. As the niadugement did ndi turn - tip the work 
man have evidonce whereafter argument was heard and date 
was fixed for passing award . Subsequently the manage 
meni Eripcured and prayed to allow it to participato in the 
hearing tefoie passing award . Having heard the porties 
order was passed allowing the management to have its say 
before final adjudication of tho dispute . Thereafter the 
management cross -examined the workman examined earlier 
and also examined one witness On its behalf . 

3 . It is contended on behalf of the managment that in 
viow of the observation of tho Hon blo Supreme Court this 
Tribunal ought to have allowed it to lead evidence both on 
the question of maintainability of the reference a well as 
on merit. That having not been done all the issues involved 
in the reforence can not be answered in either way . I am 
pot prepared to accept such contention of the management 
for the reasons set-out below , 


6 . On A conspectus of the evidence as discussed above , 
I hold that denial of employment to the workman being 
illegal and unjustified , he is entitled to reinstatement with 
full back wages . He bo reinstated and back wages be paid 
to him from the date of his removal from servico j . o ., 
7 - 9 - 79 . Payment be made within three months from the 
date of publication of publication of the Award , 

The carlier order passed by this Tribunal on 9 -3 -87 be 
treated as part of this Award .

denied employment. Tur tter was referred to the Manage 
ment and when no action was taken he raised thi9 before 
the Assistant Labour Commissioner for his action and inter 
vention . The Labour Enoiccment Officer ( C ) enquired into 
tho matter on 2 - 12 - 79 and submitted his report ( copy en 
closed as Annexure - I). 

3 . The Management s stand is that the order of reference 
made at the instance of the North Orissa Workers Union 
16 not maintainable . This Union is a foreign Union and 
is not at representative Union of the Company s workers. 
The workman in question was at no point of time in the 
employment of the Management and the question of refusal 
of his employment by the Management at any time does not 
Arise . In the early part of the year 1979 when the North 
Orissa Workers Union had raised the dispute with the 
Management , the Management vide its letter dated 19th 
October , 1979 had categorically stated and denied that the 
workers named by the said Union had at no point of time 
been in their employment. The other allegations mache in 
the written statement by the workman have been denied . 

4 . The first question for consideration is whether the 
second party was a workman under the Management. In 
this connection we have four witnesses for the workman 
and one for the Management. The Second Party has stated 
in his evidence that he was the Water Carrier under the 
First Party for about four years continuiously when his 
services were terminated as he demanded enhanced wages . 
Ho reported the fact to the Labour Officer who enquired 
into the matter, After enquiry he was given employment 
for one week only . In his crossX -examination it has been 
olicited that his duty hours were from 5 A . M , to 12 Noon 
and from 3 P . M . to 5 P . M . The witness No. 2 has similarly 
stated that the second party was working as a Water Carrier 
under the Management. This witnoss was also a workman 
under the Management. He has of course been dismissed 
from service . But that by itself would not detract from 
the value of his evidence . The witness No . 3 was also a 
workman under the Company. He has stated that the Second 
party was a Water Carrier under the First Party for about 
four years. This witness has also been chargeşhceted on 
allegation of non - performance of his duty . That fact alone 
also would not make his evidence unreliable . The Labout 
Enforcement Officcr as witness No. 4 it appears bad held 
an enquiry into the complaint made by the North Orissa 
Workers Union and during that enquiry he had recorded 
the statements of this workman and another witness vide 
Ext. 1 . This Officer no doubt looked into some of the 
records of the Company but did not find the name of the 
Worker in those register . The witness for tho Management 
has stated that the second party never worked as a Water 
Carrier under the Management. This Manager has also 
stated that the Labour Officer had inspected somo records. 
The bost evidence for the Management would have to di 
the records containing the name of this worker and to show 
that this workman was nover in their employment. In fact 
this witness in cross -oxamination has admitted that B 
form register contained all the names of the employees . 
That register if produced would havo indicated the correct 
position . For non - production of that register adverse in 
ference has to be drawn against the Management. On a 
consideration of the evidence adduced by both the partios 
I am inclined to hold that the second party was a workman 
under the First Party . This being the finding the ovidence 
of the workman that his services have been terminated has 
to be accepted . 

3. Tho next question is about the maintainability of the 
reference . The case of the Management is that the North 
Orissa Workers Union is a foreign Union and is not the 
representative of the workman . It is also said to be a 
Union not recognised by the Company . On behalf of the 
workman no evidence has been ackduced to show that tho 
workman was a member of the North Orissa Workers Union , 
It can not therefore be said that the Union could have espoused 
his cause . But the fact remains that this is a dispute as 
mentioned in tho reference between the employer and the 
workman . AS provided in Section 2 ( a ) of the Industrial 
Disputes Act the workman in case of denial of employment 
could raise their dispute individually and as such the dispute 
can not be said to bo not an industrial dispute. The reference 
is thereforo maintainable . 

6 . When considering the question of leliet it is seen that 
the parties havo neither pleaded por adduced any evidence 19

(CETT 14 - 22012/ 57/ 89 - TTT FT- II ) ] 

राजालाल , डस्क अधिकारी 


" Whether the action of the management of Deulbera 

Colliery of M /S . S . E . C . Ltd ., At / P . O . Deulvera , 
District : Dhenkanal in retiring Shri Dwari Behera , 
Mining Sirdar from service w . e .f . 16 - 7 - 1987 is 
justified ? lf not , to what relief the workman 

concerned is entitled ? " 
2 . Shortly stated the case of the workman is that he 
entered into service in Deulbera Colliery of the South 
Eastern Coalfield Ltd . on 1 - 1 - 1960. Though his actual date 
of birth as mentioned in the school leaving certificate is 
2 - 1 - 1929 but in the Official records of the management 
wrong entry has been made showing his date of birth to be 
16 - 7 - 1927 which is quite wrong and is based on no material , 
During course of his employment he appeared two examipa 
tions, namely , Mining Sirdar certificate examination and Gas 
Testing Certificate examination held on 1970 and 1972 res 
pectively . Necessary forms were forwarded by the manage 
ment before such examinations were held . Necessary entries 
of those forms including the date of birth were properly filled 
in and forwarded by the authority . Accordingly , in the 
certificates issued to the workman his date of birth has been 
mentioned as 2 - 1 - 1929. But relying on the wrong entry of 
the date of birth in the official record the Management 
passed orders that the workman would retire from service 
on attaining the age of superannuation on 16 - 7 - 1987 . So , 
the workman agitated his grievance whereafter the manage 
ment agreed that it would constitute an Age Determination 
Committee to ascertain his actual date of birth . Accordingly , 
a Committee was set-up which held its sitting on 27- 5 - 1988 
and in obsence of any material accepted the date of birth of 
the workman as mentioned in the relevant register of the 
management. Fmboldened with the finding of the Com 
mittee the management retired the workman from his service 
though infact he had some more months to serve . With 
the help of the Orissa Coalfield Labour Union he raised a 
dispute which was admitted to conciliation and the same 
having failed a reference was made to this Tribunal fur 
adjudication . It is , therefore , urred that by the time of 
llegal retirement the workman had 18 months to serve as 
because his date of birth is 2 - 1 - 1929 but not 16 - 7 - 1927 . 


New Delhi, the 6th July , 1992

3 . Refuting the claims of the workman the management 
has pleaded inter alia that the actual date of birth of the 
workman is 16 - 7 - 1927 which finds mention in the Official 
register. If actually he was born on 2 - 1 - 1929 and he was 
in possession of the school leaving certificate he should have 
produced the same when his medical examination was held 
in 1962 i. e ., after his entry into service . In the medical 
examination repor as well as in the service sheet the work 
man had put his signature wherein his date of birth finds 
mention and thereby he has acknowledged all the entries 
made therein . In this view of the matter it is pleaded that 
the workman is ertopned to say otherwise that his date of 
birth is not 16 - 7 - 1927 but 2 - 1- 1929 . Challenging the cor 
rectness of the entry of the date of birth entered in the 
subsequent two examinations, it is urged by the management 
that the same can not be accepted to he correct when there 
is earlier entry of the actual date of birth both in the medi 
cal examination report and in the seryice sheet. In şum and

The management to prove its case relies on the medi 
cal examination form , Ext. Ai, service register , Exts. B1 
and D and an application , Ext . C filed by the workman to 
return back certain docunients . In column- 6 of Ext. A / 1 
it is mentioned that from appearance , the workman was of 
about 35 years of age as on 19 - 12 - 1962 . This observation 
of the concerned doctor was merely a gucss work but not 
on the basis of any examination . In so far as service regis 
ter Ext. B / 1 is concerned , the workman has been shown 10 
huve becn born on 16 - 7 - 1927. To a court question witness 
No. 1 for the management stated that on the basis of the 
medical examination report Ext. A / 1 , date of birth of the 
workman ug aforesaid has been so mentioned in the service 
register . As observed earlier , Ext. A / 1 can not be treated 
as expert opinion based on any examination in determining 
the age of the workman . To the same effect is the value 
of Ext. D . Trlie it is , the workman has appended his signa 
ture to Ext, A / 1 but by that he can not be cstopped to 
challenge its correctness , Factually also the management is 
not correct to say that the workman was born on 16 -7 - 1927 . 
If actually the workman was 35 years of age on the date 
Ext. A / 1 was signed both by him as well as the medio 
Officer , then on inathematical calculation his date of birth 
falls on 19 -12- 1927 but not 16 - 7 - 1927 ,

6 Now coming to the evidence of the workman , I find 
that his date of birth as mentioned in his school leaving 
certificate , Ext. 1 is 2 - 1 - 1929 . The authenticity of this cer 
tificate has not been challenged by the management in couito 
of hearing The other two certificates, Exte, 2 and 3 are 
Sirdar s Certificate and Gas Testing Certificate where it is 
mentioned that the workman was born on 2 - 1 - 1929 . In 
clause ( B )(ixb ) of Ext . 4 it is provided that Mining Sirdar 
whip , Winding Engine or similar other statutory certificates 
where the Manager has to certify the date of birth will be 
11 cated as authentic . In both the certificates, Exts , 2 and 3 
referred to above , the concerned authority has certified that 
the present workman was born on 2 - 1 - 1929. On an evalua . 
tion of the cvidence of the parties, I am of the opinion that 
the cage of the workman stands in a better footing. The 
two certificates , Exls . 2 and 3 has been issued when the 
diepute regarding date of birth of the workman was not even 
in its mother s womb .

2 . The case of tho union in brief is that the 40 concerned 
workmcn named in the Schedulo of Reference were engaged 
by the omployer of Jumbad Kajora Colliery before nationali 
Sation and they worked theic under the private manageinent 
till natiopalisation and theseafter under the Eastern Coal 
fields Ltd , and their services were terminated in Fobruary , 1975 , 
I hc inanagement just to deprive the workers of their legiti 
mato benefits had been showing them as employecs recruted 
thiough contractors . But the management had no contract 
work for engagement of these workmen and there was never 
Buy licensed contractor , lhat in view of the principles luid 
down by the Hon ble Supreme Court these employces were 
the direct employees of the management. The present work 
mca were allotted work relating to mining operation and 
these works come under the prohibited category . Thc work 
men through their union approached the management to 
employ them , but the management turned deaf cars. 

The attempts of couciliation failed and the matter was 
sent to the Ministry of Labour , Government of India . Ulti 
mately the Ministry of Labour has referred the dispute to 
this Tiibunal lor adjudication . 

3 . The management of Eastern Coalfields Limited has filed 
written statement contending inter - alia that tho basic claim 
of the union is falso . The present 46 workmen wero never 
engaged by the private employer and they also never worked 
directly under the present management of Eastern Coalfields 
Limited as claimed by them . The management knows only 17 
persons numed in the Agnięxlire - I of the written statement . 
Those 17 persons were found working under contractor Sri 
Ajodhya Miytry . It is also denied that the present workmen 
were ever engaged as productive workers and their work 
cannot be considered as basic work of mining operation . Tlio 
nanagement has denied all the material averments of the 
written statement filed by the union . 

4 . In this case Sri Arun Gupta the learned Representative 
of the union has raised the following points : 
( a ) All the 46 workmen of this case were engaged by 

the employer of Jambad Kajora Colliery in January , 
1972 and they worked upto 30 -4 -73 under tho pri 

vate management . 
(b ) From 1- 5 - 73 ( the date of nationalisation ) all those 

46 workmen continued to work under Eastern Coal 
ficles Ltd ., in Şiduli Colliery and worked upto Fcb 

ruary, 1975 and then the services were torminated . 
( c ) If it is found that those 46 workmen were contract 

labours and worked for tho principal employer , tben 
they should be treated as regular employces on the 
principley laid down in the cases reported in 1990 
I.ab . I. C . page 1968 , 1988 LLJ (I) Page 730 and 
1978 I LJ(II) pago 397 .

AWARD 


The Government of India in the Ministry of Labour in 
exercise of the powers conferred on them by clause ( d ) of 
sub - section ( 1 ) and sub - section (2A ) of Section 10 of the 
Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 , has referred the following dise 
pute to this Tribunal for adjudication vide Ministry s Order 
No. L -24012 ( 154 ) / 87 - D . IV ( B ) dated the 29th April , 1988 . 


SCHEDULE 


the domestic enquiry proceedings and other relevant docu 
ments on the plea that all the documents and record of 
proceedings are missing . But in the judiciai proceeding on 
such submission the finding of the domestic enquiry cannot 
be upheld and it cannot be held that the domestic enquiry 
was properly and fairly held . As the management has fail 
ed to produce the record of the domestic enquiry proceed 
ing ; and other relevant documents . I find that the domestic 
enquiry was not properly and fairly held . I further find that 
the charge levelied against the workman were not proved by 
any cogent evidence , So the findings of the alleged domestic 
enquiry is hereby set aside . 

9 . l l the result I find that the reversion order of 
Sri Manager Singh w . e . f . 6 - 10 82 was rot justified . He 
shall be given all the pecuniary benefits and other service 
benefits holding that he was not reverted to the post of 
Grade - III Clerk w . e. f. 6 - 10 82 . 
This is my award . 

N . K . SAHA , Presiding Officer

" Whether the action of the Management of Nutandanga 

Colliery of M / s . E . C . Lid ., P . O . Nutandanga . Diss

2 . The case of the union in brief is that all the 11 work 
men of this case were continuously working at Nutandanga 
Colliery under Eastern Coalfields Ltd ., in underground jobs 
as deployed by the local administration through contractors 
both before and after take over of the non -coking coal mines 
and continued to be so deployed till 1973 . Such deployment 
of workers on jobs undergrounds connected with the coal 
production was violative of the provisions of the Contract 
Labour (Regulation & Abolition ) Act, There was serious hue 
and cry from trade unions and as well as political part es in 
Parliament. The Government decided to abolish the coniragi 
labour system and the contractors workmen were taken on 
the roll of the company sometime in 1974 . In Nutandanga 
Colliery there were some other workers working under con 
tractors and they were taken on the roll of the company . 
But for the reasons best known to the company the present 
11 concerned workmen were left out. 

The attempts of conciliation ended in failure . The matter 
was sent to the Ministry of Labour, Government of India , 
Ultimately the dispute has been referred to this Tribunal for 
adjudication . 


[ eMeT 38 – 24012/ 154/87 – 57 IV (at ) ] 

T57 TTT , FF FFTTT 


3 . There was no reason for such discrimination done by 
the management in case of the present 11 workmen . So the 
union has prayed that the present workmen may be absorbed 

the roll of the management w . e . f. 1st April , 1974 and 
they may be paid the arrcar wages and consequential bene 


fits .

4 . The management has filed written statement contending 
inter - alia that the present Reference is not maintainable . The 
workmen never worked under the management. There was 
no employer and employee relationship . So the question of 
regularisation of the present workmen does not arise . The 
present workmen were the employees of different contractors 
and were engaged for various jobs as per work order and 
terms of contract .

5 . It is the clear case of the Union that the present work 
men were the contract labours under contractors at Nutan 
danga Colliery from before nationalisation of non - cok ng 
coal mires and they continued to be so deployed even after 
nationalisation . This fact has not been challenged from the 
side of the managernent. On the other hand in para 7 of the 
written statement of the management it has been clearly 
admitted by the manarement that the present workmen werd 
the employees of diffeiept contractors engaged for various 
jobs as per work order and terms of contract. So considering 
the inaterials on record and the facts and circumstance , I 
have no heritation to find that the prerent workmen were 
never the direct emrloyees of the management both before 
and after nationalisation . But I find that the present workmen 
worked as contract labours for various jobs underground 
continuously from before nationali- ation and they continued 
to E co poplavad even after nationalisation ,

9. 1 After having heard at length both the parties, the 
following issues come up ior decision . 
( 1 ) Whether the charges levelled aga nst the workman 

are vague , baseless o - the same are really serious 

and grave ? 
( 2 ) Whether the domestic enquiry has been properly 

held and or whether principles of natural justice 

have been observed during the course of enguiry ? 
(3 ) Whether the charges levelled against the workman 

are proved beyond doubt ? 
(4 ) Whether the cornpetent aythority actent properly i. e . 

whether there was fair play in awarding punish 
ment ? 


12. Issue No. 2 -- Reasoning -- Dec sion : 

12 1 Issue No . 2 : It is known that there has been no 
statutory provisions relating to procedure to hold the domestic 
enquiry by the employers in dealing with cases of misconduct 
alleged against their employees though need not conform w 
all the judicial proceed ngs , they must however satisfy essen 
tials of natural justice . I he Supreme Court laid down guiding 
principle through judicial pronouncements , the saire is that 
enquiry should be conducted with scrupulous regard from 
requirement of rules of natur , justice that is without bisa 
and by giving the del.nquent employee an opportunity for 
adequately representing nis case and that the enquiry must 
be held honestly and bonafide with a view to deterinine 
wheher the charge against eniployee concerned is proved cr 
not , the enquiry does no recome an empty formality . The 
pr nciples of natural justice over periods, is now crystalised 
into four principles of justice ( 1962 II LLJ. Cal. ) name y : 
" (i) Opportunity for both the contesting parties to be 

heard ; 
( ii ) Hearing before impartial tribunal so that no man 

can be a judge of his own cause; 
( ) Decision made in good faith ; and 
( iv ) An orderly course of procedure . 

Apart from this , no other prirciple of natural just.cz 
is known in modern jurisprudence ." 

(1962 II LLJ. Cal.) . 
12 . 2 The Supreme Court observed in case of Sur Enamel 
and Stamping Works Ltd . ( 1963 II LLI ) as under : 
" An enquiry cannot be said to have been prcperly held 

unless 
(i) tht employee proceeded aga nst has been in or 

med clearly of the charges levelled aga nst h m , 
(ii ) Witnesses are examined ordinarily in the presence 

of the employee -- - 11 respect of charge , 
( ii ) The employee is given a fair opportunity to 

examine wi nesses, 
(iv ) He is given a fair opportunity to cross examine 

witnesses . 
( v ) He is given a fair opportunity to examine witnesses 

including himself in his efence if he so desi. es 

on any relevant matter , and 
(vi) The Enquiry Officer records his findings with ro 

asons for the same in his report." 


(5) Whether any interference by Arbitrator is called for ? 
10 . Issues - - Decision - Resoning : 

10 .1 Issue No. 1 . It is sevealed from the proceedings of 
domestic enquiry that this workman abused his super or 
Executive Engineer , and behaved in disorderly manner and 
that particulars of abus ve language was specifically given in 
the charge and I find it is not vague charge . The workinan 
nowhere denied on this count , nor he made contention that 
the charge was vague. 


10 . 2 On charge of riotons and disorderly behaviour of the 
workinan , I observe that there has been mention in charge 
sheet this specific charge . The behaviour on this court was 
not caused by any provocat on by the officer concerned i.e . 
Executive Engineer. This was not case of the workman that 
there was any provocation to him . It is nowhere stated that 
the concerned Executive Engineer had indulged in any be 
baviour which might have caused provocation in workman s 
subord nate behaviousr , aisorderly hehaviour or riotuos be 
haviour (of the workman ) . The evidence on record shows 
that this workman threatened the superior officer and he , 
therefore , cannot escape the consequences of the plea 
that it was done in fit if anger See Dhragandhra Chemical 
Works 1963 LLJ 2 . This was argued by workman at one 
stage . 


10 . 3 There has been available specific evidence , as is 
found enquiry proceedings before me, connecting th s work 
man the act of disorderly behaviour of holding of hand by 
force and pulling officer from one place to other in office 
itself in presence of cther en . ployecs and the workman 
abused the officer , the specific language nised by workman was 
recorded by witnesses who were cros -examined by the work 
man . 


12. 3 When I apply the above test to find out the status 
of the domestic enquiry , I find that thc WC. Ltd . hold 
proper enquiry and that the entire proceedings were held 
keeping in view of the principles of natural justice . The woik 
man advanced pleadings that there has been inconsistencies 
and the enquiry was biased and was not proper . After having 
pleaded this the workman did not show or point out the 
specific inconsistencies nor the workman brought out any 
mater al to show that the enquiry was not proper . The work 
man made a passing observation as above but did not bring 
out any ma erial in support of his statement. Flis statements 
at W - I and W - II does not come to h s rescue to give him 
prop . The workman did not advance any argument on this 
court. I find the enquiry was proper and it has been n ac 
cordance with the prineiples of natural justice and it is also 
in accordance with other judicial pronouncement,

13 . Issue No. 3 - Reasoning : 

13 . 1 I ne workman submitted that he did not act in riotous, 
disorderly or indecent manner though he was charged 
( Charge No. 1 ) . The enquiry proceedings reveal that ihe 
Winesies duly cross examinea confirmed that the workman 
shouted at superior officer i. c . Executive Engineer in piesepce 
of othe . employees when ther was no provocation from ihe 
officer concerned . This is sufficient to say that the work 
man s behaviour was undecent and it was not oiderly behavi 
our . The den .al of workman on this count do not help work 
man and abscive him of ine charge . The charge No. 3 
against the workman was proved to the extent that ihe work . 
man threatened , abused superior . This has been confirmed by 
the witnesses duly cross examined by the workman , thal 
abuses were hurled (by workman ) at Executive Engincer . It 
has becn very undesirable of the worknian to buil abuses , 
without any provocation , though even provocation does not 
absoive hun of this charge . This was unbecoming of any 
workmin -employee of any organisat on . It cannot be said 
that the workman was innocent. 

13. 2 Since the Enquiry Officer has not relicd on information 
given by Shri Vijay it canno : be said that principles of 
natural just ce were not followed . I he version of the work 
man on this count is not accept.ible . I reject it . 

13 . 3 The issue No. 3 is ans.vered that charges levelled 
against workman are proved beyond doubt. 


14 .5 One it is establ shed that the charges are proved , tha 
question arises whether than punishinent awardeci is justified 
or not. I find no employee worth the name has a place in 
any organisation if he is found guilty of gross misbehav our . 
No officers can take work froin their subordinate if gross 
Duisbehaviour is not condoned or no action is taken on this 
sort of behaviour . As airzady mentioned in previous paras, 
there was no provocation to workman who abused in pre 
sence of other employees n filthy language the oficer - Super 
Visor- Executive Eng neer . Tht abuses have been spelled out 
by the winesses . The affected officer lodgedi coinplaint in 
writing of such insulting behaviour or the part of this work 
man . This cciaplainant was cross examined by ths work . 
man . It cannot be imagined that othcer of status of Execu 
tive Engineer will even give a false report or complaint 
which is involving hs own insult . I find the dismissal (of 
workman ) by General Manager, W . C . Ltd , Nagpur Aica , 
Nagpur is jus ified it being fair one . The dismissal onder 
datcd 17 -5 - 1990 issued by General Manager, W . C . Ltd ., 
Nagpur Area , Nagpur as referred to in the reference tefore 
me, is just fied . Once the dismissal is found justificd , I 
answer the reference accordingly. Once I find the disim .ssal 
of workman jus ified , there is no question of grant of any 
relief on this count. 

14 .6 It has been pleaded that the award of dismissal is dis 
proportionate and or ¢ is shockingly harsh . The workinan 
harped on provisions of Section 11 - A of ID . Act , 1947 which 
gives power to Labour Courts , Tribunals and National 
Tribunals to give relief to the dismissed workmen . Tile title 
of the Sect on 11- A I . D , Act indicates that Arbitrator is 
excluded from operation of Section 11 - A . This being position , 
I am not able to act in terms of Section 11 - A , My poveis aie 
limited to the reference before me. In view of this legal 
position I am unable to gve any relief to the workman con 
cerned . 

14 .7 I have already answered the reference on affirmative . 
Hence my award .

2 . The case of the spongorin union , Bihar Colliery 
Kamgar Union , as disclosed in the writton statement submitted 
on behalf of the concerned workmen , details apart, is as 
follows : 

Gobardhan Mahato and 296 other concernca workmen 
whose sames and address have been disclosed in the annexurc 
to the schedule of reference, have been working as slurry 
removal workpien continuously to Sudamdih Coal Washery 
sinco long with unblemishod record of service. They have 
been working in permanent and prohibited category of job 
of romoval of slurry in Sudamdih Goal Washery continuously 
and in the process have put in attendance for 240 days in 
oach calendar year. They have been working on the job of 
removal of slurry within the precinct and premises of the 
washery under the supervision and control of the manage 
meot, and all implements for execution of the job are being 
supplied by the management. Although they are working 
under the management, they are being paid much less wages 
than prescribed in N . C . W . As and that too through alleged 
infermodiaries. The alleged intermediaries have neither any 
power to engage any workman nor remove any workman 
from service nor have they any power to supervise and 
control over the job of the concerned workmen . The alleged 
intermediaries have also no powor to prcscribe the requisito 
work load for tho concerned workmen .. As a matter of fact 
the requisite work load of the concerned workmen havo 
been prescribed and settled by the management. Slurryfs 
- othing but fine particles of good quality of washed " coal 


which flows out of the washery and settles down in the 
ponds tanks excavated by the managerieat. Hon blo 
Cupreme Court has held that slurry is nothing but coal. The 
concerned workmen Aso working in the ponds tanks with 
the implements supplied by the management. In the course 
of performanco of their duties they are excavating fine parti 
cles of washed coal and stacking the same near the ponds 
tanks . The fino particles of coal SO Oxcavated by then 
are being loaded on to the trucks by the management s pay 
leader through management s permanent workmen . Slurry 
so loaded in the trucks are despatched to different buyers 
of good quality of washed coal. Though the job of removal 
of slurry is a permanent nature of job cven the management 
has been paying them less wages than precribed in 
N . C . W . A . in order to victimise them an deprive them of 
their legitimato claim . As per Central Wage Board Re 
commendations and National Coal Wage Agreements the 
Stackers, for doing the job of stacking only get Group - III 
wages. The slurry cleaning workmen of Giddi Washery aro 
getting Group - III wages for performing the same mature of 
job . Despite this fact, the management hug heen depriving 
the poor workmen of their legitimate wages and claim of 
attendant benefits as available as per Central Wage Board 
Recommendations and National Coal Wage Agreements . As 
per settled law , the management is legally bound to depart 
mentalise them and 10 pay them Group - IJI wagce with re 
trospective offoct with all arrears of wages. The concerned 
workmen and their union represented for their departmen

. 3. The case of the management of Sudamdih Coal Washery 
of Ms. B . C . C . Ltd., P . O . Sudamdih , Distt. Dhanbad , as dis 
closed in the written statemcat- cum - rejoinder, benefit of de 
tails , is as follows : 


The present reference is bad in law and pot maintainable , 
as there exists as relationship of employer and employee bet 
ween the management and the persons referred to in the ro 
ference . 


The prosent reference is also in violation of the provisions of 
the Contract Labour (Regulation and Abolition ) Act and caseu 
on misrepresentations on fucts . The substantive case of wo 
management is that Sudamdih Coal Washcry is ono of tho 
Cool Washerics of M /s . B . C . C . Ltd . which is a Governinent 
Company within the moaning of Sec . 617 of the Company s 
Act and it is wholly financed by the Central Government. 
The Conl Washery in question like all other Coal Washerios 
have been constructed for the beneficiation of coal ( improving 
the quality of coal by removing ash , moisture etc .) and to 
make it suitable for use in the steel plants for steel melting . 
Tho process of washing /beneficiation of coal in washeries is 
such that some fine particles of coal flow out along with the 
special liquid solution used for the purpose of washing coal , 
This is culled slurfy which is collected in the ponds situated 
outside the washery premises and the water which is a cons 
tituent of slurry is removed and the fine particles of coal are 
salvaged and stacked . When these fine particles of coal flow 
ing out of the washery with slurry assume below a given gize / 
quality the slurry is called tailings or rejects . Since fine par 
ticles of coal and their quality above specified size represent 
washed coal which can be used in steel plants , the manage 
ment of washeries makes constant efforts / endeavour by instal 
lation of necessary equipment and modernising the equipment 
and taking various other methods having a bearing on the re 
of coal and quality as a part of blurry and to eliminate tho 
levant technology to reduos flowing out of such line particles 
samo as far as possible . When the ash content and other im 
purities in the composition of slurry exceed a specified norm , 
the slurry assumes the form of tailings /rejects and it is no 
longer necessary to collect it . Sudamdih Coal Washery was 
constructed in 1980 through & contractor on a turn -key con 
tract basis . At the time of construction of the Wasbory somo 
workers were employed by the contractor. When tho construc 
tion of the washery wag completed , there was no longer any 
work for the contractor or his men . At that time, Shri A . K . 
Roy was the M . P . of Dhanbad having been elected in 1979 
General Elections, he was also the President of tho sponsor 
ing union , Bihar Colliery Kamgar Union and he is still the 
Prosident. He launched an agitation with demonstrations etc . 
beforo the Dy. Commissioner , Dhanbad , demanding provision 
of means of livelihood of the ex -workers of the contractor 
of Sudimdih Washery Project, on the ground that they were 
local Adivasis . Th¢ workers were retrenched by the Contrac 
tor who were cngaged in the job of construction and fabri 
cation of the Washery Project. The then Dy. Commissioner , 
Dhanbad intervened in the matter and at his instance and 
on the demand of Shri A . K . Roy , the management of M / 9 , 
D . C . C . Ltd , Agreed to provide the job of siurry cleaning / 
removal work at Sudamidih Washery to some of the es -wor 
kors of the ex -contractor subject to workers forming 


a Co -operativo Society . The formation of Labour Co 
Operative was also commended by the National con 
mission of Labour appointed by the Central Govern 
ment in 1969 . In pursuance of the said arrangement the ex 
workers of ox -contractors formed a Co -operative Society 
tyled Kamgar Shramik Sahyog Samity which was duly regis 
tered under Bihur Co - operative Societies Act, 1935 . Tho 
Society obtained a liccoce under the Contract Labour ( Regu 
Jation and Abolition ) Act, 1970 and a regular contract agree 
incnt had been cxecuted between the management of M / s. 
B . C . C . Ltd . and the Co -operative Society concerned and it 
was extended / renewed from time to time till now , When the 
B . C .KU . raised the purported chisptuę before the Asstt , 
Labour Commissioner. Dhanbad in connection with another 
identical matter in September , 1986 , the union stated that 
the persons concerned were the workers of Co -operative 
Society and thut the advisory Board set up under Section 10 
of the Contract Labour (Regulation and Abolition ) Act had 
not recommended that the contract system to the job of 
slurry cleaning / removal should be prohibited . The dispute 
ruiseul by B . C . K . U . relates to the abolition of the contract 
systein in sluity removing work in Sudamdih Washery and 
departmentalisation of the work and ubsorption of the workers 
of the Co -operative Society which has been performing tho 
work / executing the contract. It is well settled that the 
demand for abolition of the contract system in any particular 
job or establishment cunnot constitute tho subject matter of 
an industrial dispute and thus such claims have to be dealt 
with under Section 10 of the Contract Labour ( Regulation 
and Abolition ) Act as per the decisions of Hon ble Supreme 
Court and other High Courts . The very same union earlier 
raised an industrial dispute regarding thọ vory samo matter 
in which it claimed that Anil Kumar Bid and 252 others 
engaged in excavation , stacking and leading of slurry in 
Sudamdin Coal Washery should be regularised in the em 
ployment of the management. That issue formed part in 
Reference No . 62 of 1989 which was tried and disposed of 
by this Tribunal. In connection with the aforesaid matter 
covered by Reference No . 62 of 1989 it so happened that 
the union claimed employment only for 118 workers , namely , 
Anil Kumar Bid and 117 others as referred to in Annexure 
* A of the written statement of the union in that case . In 
that very case , the union admitted that Haradhan Mahato 
and 14 others had already been provided employment by 
the management and gave up their casc . The union had 
also given up the case in respect of 140 workers covered 
by the said reference . The Tribunal , in Reference No. 62 
of 1989 , directed regularisation of Anil Kumar Bid and 116 
others cxcept Satrughan Mondal. It so happened that as 
an after thought and with ulteriçr motive , the Kamgar 
Shramik Sahyog Samiti has inflated the number of workers 
purported to be working in clurry removal work although 
there is absolutely no justification for such huge number of 
workmen . They never worked at the time of construction 
of washery , The management had carried out Industrial 
Engineering Studies and had come to the conclusion that 
according to the technolozy and equipment then existing , 
there would be no requirement for more than 200 workers. 
A specific reference to this state of affairs and the contentions 
of the management was made in Reference No . 62 of 1989 , 
The contention of the management was that Kamgar Shramik 
Sahyog Samiti in the mean - tme had inflated tho number of 
workers purported to be working in slurry lemoval work 
although there isubsolutely no justification for such a 
huge number of workers . The managoment carried out

Itquire oven 200 workers . Apart from 253 persons covered

rassing on the burden to the public vector management.

10 . Shri Ghosh has outlined the function of the washery 
in his earlier testimony before this Tribunal in Reference 
No. 62 ot 1969. He has stated that the function of washery 
is to wash raw coal for supply to steel plant and during 
the process of washing the quantity of ash content in coal 
is reduced and other qualities of coal improved as substance 
of energy . The principal function of the washery as underlined 
by Shri Ghosh has not been assalled . 

It appears that there is some dispute between the 
parties arrayed as to what is called slurry . According to the 
management, in the process of washing /beneficiation of coal 
in the washery , some fine particiles of coal flow out alon 
with some spçcial liquid solution used for the purpose of 
washing coal and this is calla slurry which is collected in 
the ponds situated outside the washery premises and the water 
which is a constituent of slurry lg removed and fine partic es 
of coal are salvaged and stacked . When these fine particles 
flowing out of the washery with slurry asslimo below a given 
size / quality , the slurry is called tailipgy or rejects . 

According to the uplon , slurry is nothing but ſino particles 
or yond quality of washed coal which flows out of the wash 
ery and attice down in the ponds /tanks excavated by the 
management. 

Shri Indrajit Ghosh in his earlier testimony in Rcterence 
No 62 of 1989 stated that in the process of washing coal 
in he wa hery, cluent comes out of the wachcry . Efluent 
consists of water , fine particles of coal and sandy particles, 
The composition of cffluent varies from timo to time, but 
the total percentage of solid out of effluent is about 25 per 
cent and effluent goes down to eliuent ponde called also the 
SIITTY ponds. The solid portion of coal settles down in the 
nond and the watery portion is reveled back to the wacher 
ind the solid portion which is settled down in the pond is 
excavated . In cross - examination , he has admitted that wash 
rry slurry and washery effluent are interchangeable terms : 
he has also admitted that the management has not filed any 
document to cstablish the fact as to the composition of total 
rercentage of solid in the gluity . The management has not 
olso disclosed the percentage of composition of solid in the 
slurry in its written statement. Anyway , from the evidence 
on record , it is established that in the process of washing of 
coal in the washery effluent or slurry comes down which tv 
composed of fine nårticles of coal , Vater and sandy particles 
and this cffluent or slurry is deposited in the luftv ponds 
The evidence on record reveals that the procese of washing of 
coal in the coal washery lo necessary for beneficiation of 
coal in order to make the game suitable for use in the steel 
plants hy reclucing the Ash content and improving ortic 
qualities of coal as substance of corgy .

the management . It has been held by Hon ble Supremo 
Court that when a worker or group of woj crs labours 10 
fender service which forms integral part of service of 
1 ,10ther and utilised as such , then the other is , in fact , the 
employer oven though the employment is brought about by 
or through some other person acting under thic supervision 
And control of the former ( 1978 Lab . I. C . 1264 between 
Hussainbhai Vs. The Alath Factory Tezhilali Union and 
others). This position has been relied on by Calcutta High 
Court in tho case reported in 1991 Lal) . I . C . 1062 ( Divi . 
sional Railway Manager , Eastern Railway Vs. Satyajit 
Mazumdar ). This being the legal position , I have no hesi 
tation to hold that the concerned work mon are the real 
workpien of the management of Sudandil Cual Washery . 
This being so , there exists relationship of oployer and 
employoe between the management of Sudandih Coal Wash 
ry and the concerned workmen ,

13 . The I partly General Secretary of Dena Bank Emplo 
yees Union ( W . W . 1 ) has stated in his evidence that the 
workman Devaraj told him that the letter was obtained from 
him when he was in his house . No doubt he has stated that 
he has got knowledge of the letter . This is not sufficient in 
view of the say of W . W . 1 that Devaraj told him that the 
letter was obtained from him when he was in his house . 


14 . From the discussiou made in the above paras I am of 
opinion , the alleged letter given by workman Devaraj has 
not been proved . So I hold additional issue No . 1 against 
the II party and additional issue No. 2 in favour of the I 
party . The reference is maintainable and cannot be rejected 
as contended in para 13 of the counter statement. The 
action of the II party management and the workman Devaraj 
have no relevance to the present dispute since the letter has 
not been proved . 


5 . The I party , on 8th August, 1989 , has filed the rejoin 
der In the rejninder he has reaffirmed his case set out in the 
claim statement . It is stated in the rejoinder that the letter 
dated 13th February , 1989 referred to by the II party was 
taken by the workman Devaraj by some top officers by visit 
ing the house of Devaraj. The Union is the l party and the 
offer should have been made to the Union . The IT party 
cannot put an end to the present reference by questionable 
methods. 1 he request of the worhman to withdraw the dispute 
has no relevance because the dispute has been raised by the 
Union . 

6 . My Learned Predecessor has framed the following two 
additional issues : 
1 . Whether the reference is not maintainable and is 

reliable to be rejecled for the reasons shown in para 
13A of the counter statement ? 
Whether the action of the It party management 
and the concerned employee have no relevance to the 
dispue and whether the reference is still maintainable 
as contended by the I party in para 5 of the te 

joinder ? 
7 . On behalf of the II party no evidence has been adduced 
( See order sheet datcd 27th November , 1990 ) . On behalf of 
the I rarty W . W . 1 Jayakumar, General Secretary of the Den 
Bank Emplovees Union has been examined . Exs , M . 1 to 8 
are marked through W . W . 1 . These documents were produced 
by the II party . 

8 . The memorandum dated 27th July , 1987 issued to the 
workman Devaraj, unfortunately not marked , is not disputed . 
Its zerox copy is before the Tribunal. As per this order in the 
memorandum the workman has been debarred for a period of 
3 years from holding any allowance carrying post as per the 
settlement because he refused to accept the offer to perform 
cash - peon duties at Peenya Branch , 


15 . Now the point for consideration is whether the II party 
Bank was justified in debariiug the workman Devaraj , Cleaner 
cum - Sepoy from holding any special allowance carrying post 
for a period of 3 years as per the letter dated 27 - 7 - 1987 . 
It is not disputed hat if a cleaner - cum - sepoy refuses to accept 
the post of cash -reon is liable to be debarred for 3 years from 
holding any special allowance carrying post. It is not dis . 
puted that the workman Devaraj refused to accept the most 
of cash -peon when he was offered . 

16 . Ex. M . 6 is the Seniority list of the subordinato staff , 
This shows the seniority of cleaner - cum -sepoy . In the senin 
rity list . the present workman Devavraj stands at No . 28 . 
Ahove hiri there are four persons belonging to the category 
of the workman Devaraj. They are Mannan (SI. No . 24 ), 
Udayakumar ( SI, No . 25 ). K . Venkataiah ( Sl. No 26 ), and 
Gopalan ( S1 , No . 27 ) . Wlien the vacancy of cash peon at 
Peenya arose , it is most significant to note , it was not offered 
to the said four senior persons but it was offered to workman 
Devaraj. The conduct of the II party shows, as rightly con 
tended on behalf of the I party , that it was offered to work 
man Devaraj because Peenya was far off from the residence 
of the workman . The lack of bonafides in the offer made ty 
the [I party to the workman Devaraj is writ larg from the 
records . The workman Devaraj cannot be penalised for re 
fusing the offer of cash peon post. 

17 . For the aforesaid reasons the reference has to be 
accepted . 

18 . All other documents and evidence not referred to by 
me are not relevant. In any case they do not alter the conclu 
sions reached by nie above . I have touched the salient 
rcints . 


9 . At the outset it is argued by the Learned Counsel for 
the Il party that the reference is not maintainable since the 
period of three years mentioned in his memorandum has elaps 
ed . When the reference lias been ma e , the Tribunal is bound 
to pass its award , rejecting the reference or accepting the 
reference . If an award is passed in favour of the workman , 
he will be entitled to some benefits like back wages . So there 
is no force in the argunent that the reference is not main 
tainable . 


AWARD 


The management of Dena Bank was not justified in debtr 
ring the workman Devaraj. Cleaner - cum - Sepoy from holding 
any special allowance carrying post for a period of 3 years 
as per the letter dated 27 - 7 - 1987. 


The workman is entitled to the special allowance if any 
which he was getting in his post cleaner - cum - sepoy . 


10 . The main argument advanced by the Learned Counsel 
for the II party is that the reference is not maintainable and 
should be rejected because the workman Devaraj has given 
a letter to the II party stating that he would withdraw the 
reference before this Tribunal. In this regard the Learned 
Counsel rclied on the decision of our Hon ble High Court re 
ported in FIR 1982 Page 148 (Mysore Structurals Ltd ., v / s . 
Workmen of Mysore Structurals I td . ) . In this case his Lord 
ship the Hon ble Mr. Justice K . A . Swamy has been pleased 
to lay down that even if the workman is represented by the 
Trade Union (as in this case ), the right of the workman to 
settle his dispute himself with the employer is not taken 
away . The Learned counsel for the I party stated this deci 
sion was not applicable to the facts of the case . But he was 
unable to say how it was not applicable . It is clear from the 
Law laid down by our Hon ble High Court that the workman 
himself can settlement the dispute before the Tribunal, though 
his cause has been championed by the Trade Union . 

11 . But there is great difficulty for this Tribunal to apply 
the law laid down by our Hon ble High Court and give a 
finding in favour of the II party . 

12 . The alleged letter given by the workman Devaraj to 
the II party has been produced before this Tribunal by the 
II party . But it has not been marked and it has not been 
proved according to Law . It was obligatory on the part of 
the II party to examine a proper person to get the letter 
marked and prove it . That has not been done . This alleged 
letter has not been unequivocally admitted by the I party . 
in the rejoinder fjeld by the party it has been stated that 
the I [ party connot put an end to the dispute before this 
Tribunal by using questionable and objectionable methods. It 
has been stated that the top Officers of the II party went to 
the house of workman Devaraj and got the alleged letter , 
This obviously means that the I party has repudiated the lette : 
alleged to have been given voluntarily by the workman Deva 
Taj to the Il praty . 


Reference accepted as stated herein . 
No costs ,

11. Upor consideration of evidence on record , I 
come to the conclusion that the concerned workman 
was not guilty of theft , fraud or dishonesty in connec 
tion with compapy s business or property. There is 
no cvidence on record to show that he did anything 
with intention of causing wrongful gain to himself 
or wrongful loss to this employer . There is also no 
evidence on record that he had intention to deceive 
his employer. But nevertheless the fact remains that 
he committed some mistakes and carelessness which 
he should have avoided . 

12 . The sponsoring union has claimed reinstate 
ment of the concerned workman in service . From niy 
discussion above it is evident that the punishent of 
dismissal from service awarded to the concerned 
workman must be set aside . But the question re 
mains if the concerned workman should be rein 
stated in service with full back wages . The effect of 
reinstatement is to restore an employee to his fos 
mer caapcity , s atus, emoluments and continuity of 
service , as if his services had never heen terminated . 
In the absence of cogent reason to the contrary , hc 
is entitled to compensation which should normally 
be equal to full wage . In the present case siror the 
conccned workian committed mistake and care 
lessness , he is not entitled to full wages for the 
period he remained dismissed from service , In my 
view end of justice will be not if he is allowed 500% 
of his hack whors from the date of his dismissal 
from service till he is reinstated 


Your about intentional wilful acts and omissions 
constirute misconduct of fraud and dishonesty and 
von are hehehy charged under clause 2712 ) of the 
certified standing orders which read as follows : 
" Theft, fraud or dishonesty in concerned with 

the company s business or property."

2 . Both the references have been heard together

main point for consideration would be as to whether 
the concerned workmen can be treated as clay cart

7. The concerned workmen of all the respective 
collieries claim to be the permanent employees of 
BCCL and they further claim to have been preparing 
clay cartridges which are used by the management 
for blasting purpose . In this context first of all we 
will examine the feas bility as to how an individual 
can be in a position not being an employee of the 
colliery , to supply clay cartridges (w 1000 per day . 
Amulya Bouri WW - 1 has stated that he is in a 
position to prepare clay cariric ges at least 1000 per 
diy per head . This means il man can prepare 1000 
clay cartridges per day for its supply to the manuge 
ment. As per evidence size of the clay cartridges is 
6 " long with 1 " diameter, A piece of clay cartridges 
has also been proved as nuterial Ext. 1 . Now the 
crux of the question is as to how an individual can 
be a regular supplier of 1000 clay cartridges per day . 
It will require u : least severalm ets of clay of every 
dav . Had this been 1 business of a few days only 
that could have been arranged by digging up mud 


3 . The common claims of the concerned workmen 
in both reference are that they are permanent cm 
ployecs of BCCL engaged in respective collicries in 
preparing clay cartridges which are used for blasting 
in coal seams for production of coal, They claim to

from the land of others or from own land . It can be 
well innagined that regular excavation of earth from 
a piece of land will convert the land into a big tank 
only within a few months. It is not possible that a 
land owner will allow anybody to take out earth from 
his land continuously for month together. This imeans 
a supplier of clay cartridges will be required to dig 
his own land converting it into a tank in no time 
and for no benefit. There is evidence that Amulya 
Bouri has been supplying clay cartridges everyday and 
getting Rs. 200 /- per month . The question is as to 
who will be the fool on this earth to spoil the fertility 
of land for no gains. This lone circumstance appears 
to be udrebutable in nature and is suggestive of the 
fact that the clay cartridges makers, whosoever he may 
be has been using the land of the colliery and within 
the premises of the colliery itself . The natural consc 
quence will follow that the management will supply 
the required material inclusive of water to accomplish 
the job . So I am to hold that the preparation of clay 
cartridges at hoinc by the suppliers is not possible 
at all . In other words the supplier or the workmen 
have been preparing clay cartridges at the land of 
the management and that too with the help of the 
management. From these facts it also becomes evi 
dent that the work of the clay cartridges maker will 
be supervised by the authorities concerned of the 
collieries . This means that there was control and 
supervision of the management,

showing preparation of bills in the name of Amulya 
Bouri .
